and
\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{P}(O)
were the original expressions we derived, and follow much more directly from the likelihood factorization in Equation (2). The presence of density ratio
\frac{\lambda_{1}(\bm{L}^{e}_{m};\bm{L}^{*})}{\lambda_{0}(\bm{L}^{e}_{m};\bm{L}^{*})}
makes nonparametric estimation more challenging given that both
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
and
\bm{L}^{*}
can be multidimensional and in general, conditional density estimation is a very challenging statistical problem. While other techniques for density ratio estimation exist
(
Sugiyama et al., 2012
)
, our reparameterization follows the same technique as
Díaz et al. (2023)
.

When estimating
\theta(P)
using a one-step estimator for
\beta(P)
based on this alternative representation of
\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)
or
\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{P}(O)
, we denote such estimators by
\dot{\beta}_{\widetilde{P}}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{P}}(O)
, respectively, and corresponding estimators for
\theta(P)
by
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
. A choice to use
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
rather than
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
or
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
might be more appropriate if analysts had understanding of the exact process giving rise to eligibility defining covariates
\bm{L}^{e}
. For completeness, we provide summary of the asymptotics of these alternative estimators in Section
S3.5
.

S2.2
Correspondence Between
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
and One-Step Estimation of the ATT

In the absence of missing eligibility data,
R=1
for all subjects. Suppose further that all
n
remaining subjects are eligible for the study
(E=1)
. Such might be the case if analysis is restricted to
n
eligible subjects in an EHR database with
N>n
subjects. This is analogous to the typical way cohorts are built in observational studies for point exposures if missing eligibility criteria is not an issue. We will show informally that when
(R=1,E=1)
for all
n
subjects on which we observe data units
(O_{1},...,O_{n})
,
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
simplifies to the usual one-step estimator for the average treatment effect on the treated (ATT). Under this scenario

•

R=1,E=1
for all subjects

•

\eta(\bm{L}^{*},A)=1
for
A\in\{0,1\}
(All
n
patients are complete cases)

•

\varepsilon_{a}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)=1
for
a\in\{0,1\}
(All
n
patients are study eligible)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]=\mathbb{P}_{n}

\displaystyle\Biggr\{\frac{AR}{\widehat{\eta}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr[\Bigr(E-\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)Y-\Bigr(E\widehat{\mu}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\widehat{\xi}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle+A\Bigr(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\widehat{\xi}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr[E\frac{\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\Bigr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle-(1-A)\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}

\displaystyle\Biggr\{-A\Bigr(\widehat{\mu}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\widehat{\xi}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)+A\Bigr(Y-\widehat{\xi}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)

\displaystyle-(1-A)\biggr[\frac{\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\Bigr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle-(1-A)\Bigr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}

\displaystyle\Biggr\{A\Bigr(Y-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-(1-A)\biggr[\frac{\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr]\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}

\displaystyle\Biggr\{\Bigr(Y-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr[A-(1-A)\biggr(\frac{\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr)\biggr]\Biggr\}

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]=\mathbb{P}_{n}

\displaystyle\Biggr\{\ A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\widehat{\eta}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)+\frac{ARE}{\widehat{\eta}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Biggr\}=\mathbb{P}_{n}[A]

Thus

\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]}=\mathbb{P}_{n}\Biggr\{\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[A]}\Bigr(Y-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr[A-(1-A)\biggr(\frac{\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\widehat{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr)\biggr]\Biggr\}

which is nothing more than the one-step estimator (eg., doubly-robust) for the ATT
(
Kennedy et al., 2015
;
Mercatanti and Li, 2014
;
Moodie et al., 2018
)
. A very similar argument yields the same result for
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
.

S3
Proofs of Theoretical Properties

S3.1
von Mises Expansion of
\alpha(P)
and
\beta(P)
with
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{*}(O)
(Proof of Lemma 1)

Before proving Lemma 1, we will note that

\displaystyle\alpha(P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\mathbb{E}_{P}[E~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,R=1,Y]\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}[A\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)]

where the last line follows by applying iterated expectation conditioning on
\bm{L}^{*},A,Y
and than applying Assumption 4 (MAR) to note that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[R~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,Y]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[R~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1]=\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)
. Similarly, we note that

\displaystyle\beta(P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\Bigr|\bm{L}^{*},A=1,R=1,Y\biggr]\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(Y\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]~~~({\text{Defn. of $\varepsilon_{a},\xi$}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigr[A\Bigr(Y\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\Bigr]

where again the last line follows by applying iterated expectation conditioning on
\bm{L}^{*},A,Y
and than applying Assumption 4 (MAR) to note that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[R~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,Y]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[R~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1]=\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)
. Now to prove Lemma 1:

\displaystyle R_{\alpha}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\alpha(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\alpha(P)+\int\dot{\alpha}^{*}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}(o)dP(o)

\displaystyle=\alpha(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\alpha(P)+\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)+\frac{ARE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)+\frac{ARE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-A\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)+\frac{AR\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-A\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\biggr]~~~\text{(It. Exp. on $\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y$)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\ \biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})+A\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-A\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr]~~~\text{(It. Exp. on $\bm{L}^{*},A,Y$ + A4)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})\biggr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle R_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\beta(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\beta(P)+\int\dot{\beta}^{*}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}(o)dP(o)

\displaystyle=\beta(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\beta(P)+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{\Bigr(E-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)Y-\Bigr(E\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}+A\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle-(1-A)\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)-\beta(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{\Bigr(E-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)Y-\Bigr(E\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}+A\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle-(1-A)\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)-A\Bigr(\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

Adding and subtracting the term
E\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})
on line 1 and applying iterated expectation (conditioning on
\bm{L}^{*},A,Y,\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
in line 2) yields

\displaystyle R_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{\Bigr(E-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)Y-\Bigr(E\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+E\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-E\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle-(1-A)\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)

\displaystyle-A\Bigr(\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)+A\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

Next, we rearrange and combine terms and begin simplifying. In particular, we apply additional iterated expectations in lines 1 (on
\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y
) and 3 (on
\bm{L}^{*},A,Y
, followed by Assumption 4) of the below expression, and finally add and subtract
\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y
in the final term below.

\displaystyle R_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\biggr\{Y\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)-\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\frac{RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\{A-(1-A)\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\}

\displaystyle+(1-A)\frac{\Bigr(\eta(\bm{L}^{*},0)-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},0)\Bigr)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

Applying iterated expectation once more (to line two, on
\bm{L}^{*},A,R,\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
), we arrive at the desired result.

\displaystyle R_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\biggr\{Y\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)-\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\frac{RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\{\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\bigr(1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\bigr)-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\bigr(1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\bigr)}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\}

\displaystyle+(1-A)\frac{\Bigr(\eta(\bm{L}^{*},0)-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},0)\Bigr)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{Y\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)-\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\biggr)\Bigr(Y(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})-(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}-\xi)\Bigr)+\frac{RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}(\mu_{0}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0})\biggr(\frac{u(1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(1-u)}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}}\biggr)

\displaystyle+(1-A)\frac{(\eta_{0}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0})}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\Bigr(Y(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}-\gamma)-(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}-\chi)\Bigr)\biggr]

This finishes the proof of Lemma 1, by showing that the claimed von Mises expansions are satisfied. An immediate consequence of Lemma 1—invoking Lemma 2 in
Kennedy et al. (2023)
—is that
\dot{\alpha}^{*}_{P}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}^{*}_{P}(O)
are influence functions at
P
in a semiparametric model induced by Assumption 4. As mentioned in the main paper, Assumption 4 restricts the tangent space of the model, and as such demonstrating that the claimed von Mises expansions are satisfied is not sufficient to prove that
\dot{\alpha}^{*}_{P}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}^{*}_{P}(O)
the
efficient
influence functions. In order to prove Theorem 2, that
\dot{\alpha}^{*}_{P}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}^{*}_{P}(O)
are indeed the efficient influence functions in the induced semiparametric model, we will first show that
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
satisfy a different set of von Mises expansions for
\alpha(P)
and
\beta(P)
, respectively, and thus are themselves influence functions (Section
S3.2
). Then, we will go through the process of projecting
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
onto the tangent space of the model, and show that projection yields
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{*}(O)
, thereby proving
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{*}(O)
are indeed the efficient influence functions (Section
S3.3
)

S3.2
von Mises Expansion of
\alpha(P)
and
\beta(P)
with
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)

Lemma S2

\alpha(P)
and
\beta(P)
satisfy the von Mises expansions

\displaystyle\alpha(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\alpha(P)

\displaystyle=-\int\dot{\alpha}^{\prime*}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}(o)dP(o)+R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle\beta(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\beta(P)

\displaystyle=-\int\dot{\beta}^{\prime*}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}(o)dP(o)+R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

where the remainder terms (omitting inputs for brevity) are as follows:

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}-\omega_{1})\biggr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\biggr)(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}-\nu)+\frac{RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}(\mu_{0}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0})\biggr(\frac{u(1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(1-u)}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}}\biggr)\biggr]

The proof of Lemma
S2
is very similar to that of Lemma 1, from the previous section. Before proving Lemma
S2
, we note that

\displaystyle\alpha(P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\mathbb{E}_{P}[E~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,R=1]\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}[A\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})]

where the last line follows by applying iterated expectation conditioning on
\bm{L}^{*},A
. Additionally, we note that

\displaystyle\beta(P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\Bigr|\bm{L}^{*},A=1,R=1\biggr]\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr]~~~({\text{Defn. of $\nu$}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}[A\nu(\bm{L}^{*})]~~~(\text{It. Exp. on $\bm{L}^{*},A$})

Now to prove Lemma
S2

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\alpha(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\alpha(P)+\int\dot{\alpha}^{\prime*}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}(o)dP(o)

\displaystyle=\alpha(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\alpha(P)+\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})+\frac{ARE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})+\frac{ARE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-A\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})+\frac{AR\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-A\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr]~~~\text{(It. Exp. on $\bm{L}^{*},A,R$)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})+A\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-A\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr]~~~\text{(It. Exp. on $\bm{L}^{*},A$)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}-\omega_{1})\biggr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\biggr)\biggr]

Next we have that

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\beta(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\beta(P)+\int\dot{\beta}_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P}}^{\prime*}(o)dP(o)

\displaystyle=\beta(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P})-\beta(P)+\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*})+\frac{ARE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-A\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*})-A\nu(\bm{L}^{*})+\frac{ARE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr]

Now rearranging terms, and applying iterated expectations, we have

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*})-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)~~~\text{(It. Exp. on $\bm{L}^{*},A,R$, then again on $\bm{L}^{*},A$)}

\displaystyle+\frac{ARE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m}))\biggr)

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\biggr)\biggr]~~~\text{(It. Exp. on $\bm{L}^{*},A,R,\bm{L}^{e}_{m})$}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*})-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)+\frac{RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\{A-(1-A)\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\Bigr(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*})-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{RE}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\Bigr(1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\}\biggr]

where the final line follows from iterated expectations on
\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m},R
. Omitting conditioning terms we see that this error term is of second order

\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\biggr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\biggr)(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}-\nu)+\frac{RE(\mu_{0}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0})}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\biggr\{\frac{u(1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u})-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}(1-u)}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{u}}\biggr\}\biggr]

This proves Lemma
S2
and shows that both
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
are valid influence functions for
\alpha(P)
and
\beta(P)
at
P
, respectively, as were
\dot{\alpha}^{*}_{P}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}^{*}_{P}(O)
. In Section
S3.3
, we will go through the process of projecting
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
onto the tangent space of the model, and show that projection yields
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{*}(O)
, thereby proving
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{*}(O)
are indeed the efficient influence functions of
\alpha(P)
and
\beta(P)
.

S3.3
Derivation of
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{*}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{*}(O)
From
\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)
(Proof of Theorem 2)

Assumption 4 (
R\perp\!\!\!\perp(Y,\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},A
) is equivalent to the conjunction of two conditions, which play different roles. Throughout,
Y
is observed for all subjects and
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
is either completely observed (
R=1
) or completely missing (
R=0
). The first condition,
R\perp\!\!\!\perp Y~|~\bm{L}^{*},A
, is a restriction on the law of the observed data
O
: it implies that the joint distribution of
(R,Y)
given
(\bm{L}^{*},A)
factorizes as
p(R~|~\bm{L}^{*},A)\,p(Y~|~\bm{L}^{*},A)
. This restriction shrinks the tangent space, so that influence functions for
\alpha(P)
and
\beta(P)
are no longer unique; this is why
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{\prime*}
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{\prime*}
are valid but not efficient. The second condition,
R\perp\!\!\!\perp\bm{L}^{e}_{m}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,Y
, places no restriction on the observed data law, since
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
is unobserved whenever
R=0
, and so it does not change the tangent space. Identification of
\theta^{\text{elig}}_{\text{ATT}}
uses Assumption 4 as a whole: together, the two conditions equate complete-case quantities such as
\mu_{0}
,
\varepsilon_{1}
and the nested nuisance functions with their full-population counterparts (Section S1). The efficiency bound in Theorem 2, however, is induced by the first condition alone. We let
\Lambda_{P}
denote the tangent space of the model, which by Lemma 24 of
Rotnitzky and Smucler (2020)
decomposes into orthogonal subspaces as follows:

\Lambda_{P}=\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{*},A}\oplus\Lambda_{R|\bm{L}^{*},A}\oplus\Lambda_{Y|\bm{L}^{*},A}\oplus\Lambda_{R\bm{L}^{e}_{m}|\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y}

where in the above

\Lambda_{W|V}=\bigr\{f(w,v)\in L_{2}^{0}(P)~|~\mathbb{E}_{P}[f~|~V]=0\bigr\}

Next, we introduce the projection operator
\Pi
. For any function
f\in L_{2}(P)
,

\Pi(f,\Lambda_{W|V})=\mathbb{E}_{P}[f(O)~|~W,V]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f(O)~|~V]

Projection of
\alpha_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
onto
\Lambda_{P}

First notice that because
A\Bigr(1-\frac{R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr)\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})
is mean zero given
\bm{L}^{*},A
, we have that

\alpha_{P}^{\prime*}(O)=\underbrace{A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})}_{\in\Lambda_{R|\bm{L}^{*},A}}+\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(P)

Thus, we only need to project the latter two terms on
\Lambda_{P}
. Furthermore, notice that term
\alpha(P)
is a constant (given the distribution
P
), it will cancel out in projection onto every orthogonal subspace with conditioning statements, and thus we need only keep track of it when projecting onto orthogonal subspace without conditioning statements.

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(P)~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{*},A,R}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(P)~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R\biggr]=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})-\alpha(P)

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(P)~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{Y|\bm{L}^{*},A}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,Y\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\mathbb{E}[E~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,Y,R=1]~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,Y\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\mathbb{E}[E~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R=1]~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,Y\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A\biggr]

\displaystyle=A\Bigr(\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\omega_{1}(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)~~~\text{(A4 and defn. of }\eta)

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(P)~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{e}_{m}|\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y,\bm{L}^{e}_{m}\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\mathbb{E}[E~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,R=1,Y]

\displaystyle=\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)

Summing terms, we have

\Pi\Bigr(\alpha_{P}^{\prime*}(O),\Lambda_{P}\Bigr)=A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)+\frac{ARE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}-\alpha(P)

which is exactly
\alpha^{*}_{P}(O)
. Thus,
\alpha^{*}_{P}(O)
is the efficient influence function of
\alpha(P)
.

Projection of
\beta_{P}^{\prime*}(O)
onto
\Lambda_{P}

We begin by presenting
\dot{\beta}^{\prime*}_{P}(O)
with terms slightly rearranged, which will facilitate the projection of terms onto the requisite orthogonal subspaces.

\displaystyle\dot{\beta}^{\prime*}_{P}(O)

\displaystyle=A\biggr(1-\frac{R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr)\nu(\bm{L}^{*})+\frac{RE}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\biggr)\biggr[A-(1-A)\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr]-\beta(P)

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}+\underbrace{A\nu(\bm{L}^{*})-\beta(P)}_{\in\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{*},A}}

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}

where
A\nu(\bm{L}^{*})-\beta(P)\in\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{*},A}
as
\mathbb{E}_{P}[A\nu(\bm{L}^{*})]=\beta(P)
. Thus we only need to project the components of the first and third term on each portion of the tangent space.

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{*},A,R}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R\biggr]-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\nu(\bm{L}^{*})-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{e}_{m}|\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}-\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{Y\mathbb{E}[E~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y]-\mathbb{E}[E\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y]-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}-\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{\Bigr(E-\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)Y-\Bigr(E\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{Y|\bm{L}^{*},A}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,Y\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[A\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,Y,R=1\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[A\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R=1\biggr]~~~\text{(\ref{lemma:bvt})}

\displaystyle=A\biggr(Y\mathbb{E}[E~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,Y,R=1]-\mathbb{E}[E\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,Y,R=1]-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr)

\displaystyle-A\biggr(\mathbb{E}\Bigr[E\bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\bigr)~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A=1,R=1\Bigr]-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\biggr)

\displaystyle=A\Bigr(\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)-A\Bigr(\nu(\bm{L}^{*})-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=A\Bigr(\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)-\nu(\bm{L}^{*})\Bigr)

Now for the final term,
\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{*},A,R}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}E\mathbb{E}[Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1,\bm{L}^{e}_{m}]~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}E\Bigr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\biggr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{\bm{L}^{e}_{m}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{Y\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R,Y\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr\{E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m}\Bigr)-\Bigr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\Pi\biggr(\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\Lambda_{\bm{Y}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A,Y\biggr]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\biggr[\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\biggr\{E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\biggr\}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A\biggr]

\displaystyle=(1-A)\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Biggr\{Y\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1,Y\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1,Y\biggr]\Biggr\}

\displaystyle-(1-A)\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Biggr\{\mathbb{E}\biggr[EY\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1\biggr]\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=(1-A)\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\biggr)

The second/third to last lines follow from Lemma
S1
. That the 2nd term disappears in the final line is because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggr[EY\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1\biggr]-\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}~\Bigr|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1,\bm{L}^{e}_{m}\biggr]~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggr[E\Bigr(\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A=0,R=1\biggr]

\displaystyle=0

Putting the pieces together we have that

\displaystyle\Pi\bigr(\beta_{P}^{\prime*}(O),\Lambda_{P}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{AR}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr[\Bigr(E-\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)Y-\Bigr(E\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle+A\Bigr(\varepsilon_{1}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\xi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-A)R}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\biggr[E\frac{u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}{1-u(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})}\Bigr(Y-\mu_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})\Bigr)-\Bigr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle-(1-A)\frac{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},0)}{\eta(\bm{L}^{*},1)}\Bigr(\gamma(\bm{L}^{*},Y)Y-\chi(\bm{L}^{*},Y)\Bigr)-\beta(P)

which is exactly
\beta^{*}_{P}(O)
. Thus,
\beta^{*}_{P}(O)
is the efficient influence function of
\beta(P)
, concluding the proof of Theorem 2.

S3.4
Asymptotic Behavior of
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
(Proof of Theorem 3)

Proof of Theorem 3

To prove Theorem 3, we follow arguments used for ratio parameters by (Kennedy et al., 2023) (Theorem 3)
(
Kennedy et al., 2023
)
and Levis et al. (Theorem 1)
(
Levis et al., 2024b
)
. For ease of notation, we use the shorthand
P[f]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[f(O)]
for the mean of any function
f
under distribution
P
. We then have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}-\theta(P)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\beta}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]}-\frac{P[\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]}{P[\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]}\biggr\{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\beta}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]-P[\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]-\theta(P)\Bigr(\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]-P[\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]\Bigr)\biggr\}

Now applying the results of the von Mises expansions in Lemma
S2
, we have the following decompositions:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\beta}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]-P[\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)[\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)[\dot{\beta}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]+R_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]-P[\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)[\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]+R_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)

Because our estimation procedure for
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
leverages sample splitting (Algorithm 1), Lemma 2 of (Kennedy, 2020) guarantees that
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)[\dot{\beta}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)]
and
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]
are both
o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

(
Kennedy, 2020
)
. Plugging in these decompositions, and using the fact that
\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]
is bounded away from zero, we have that

\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}-\theta(P)=\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)\bigr(\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)-\theta(P)\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)+O_{P}(|R_{\alpha}|+|R_{\beta}|)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

Next, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]-\alpha(P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]-P[\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)]

(S1)

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)\bigr(\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)\bigr(\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)+P\bigr(\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)

\displaystyle=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})+O_{P}(n^{-1/2})+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=o_{P}(1)

where the third equality follows from the central limit theorem (term 1), Lemma 2 of (Kennedy, 2020)
(
Kennedy, 2020
)
(term 2), and that for term 3,
\Bigr|P\bigr(\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)\Bigr|\lesssim\|\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)-\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\|=o_{P}(1)
by assumption. Recalling from Corollary 2.1 that
\dot{\theta}^{*}_{P}(O)=\frac{1}{\alpha(P)}\bigr(\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)-\theta(P)\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)
, observe that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)\bigr(\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)-\theta(P)\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)\bigr(\theta_{P}^{*}(O)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\biggr(\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]}-\frac{1}{\alpha(P)}\biggr)(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)\bigr(\dot{\beta}_{P}(O)-\theta(P)\dot{\alpha}_{P}(O)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha(P)-\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]\alpha(P)}\cdot O_{p}(n^{-1/2})~~~\text{(Central Limit Theorem)}

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1)O_{p}(n^{-1/2})~~~\text{(Equation \eqref{eqn:alpha_decomp} and $\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)]$ bounded away from zero)}

\displaystyle=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

Combining pieces, we have the desired result, that

\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{EIF}}-\theta(P)=\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\theta}^{*}_{P}(O)]+O_{P}(|R_{\alpha}|+|R_{\beta}|)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

Proof of Corollary 3.1

Since
A\in\{0,1\}
is bounded, and
P(\widehat{\eta}_{1}>\epsilon)=1
for some
\epsilon>0
, we have that

\displaystyle|R_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)|

\displaystyle=\Biggr|\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})\Bigr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}\Bigr)\biggr]\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\Bigr|\frac{A}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})(\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1})\Bigr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\|\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\|\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|~~~\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\|\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\|\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|)

Similarly, since
|Y|\leq M<\infty
almost surely, we have that

\displaystyle\Biggr|\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\Bigr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}\Bigr)\Bigr(Y(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})\Bigr)\biggr]\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\Bigr|\frac{AY}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}(\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1})(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})\Bigr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[|Y|\Bigr|(\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1})(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})\Bigr|\biggr]~~~(|A|\leq 1,\ \widehat{\eta}_{1}>\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{\epsilon}\|\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\|\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|~~~\text{($|Y|\leq M$ and Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\|\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\|\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|)

\displaystyle\Biggr|\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[-A\Bigr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}\Bigr)(\widehat{\xi}-\xi)\biggr]\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{1}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}\Bigr|(\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1})(\widehat{\xi}-\xi)\Bigr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|\|\widehat{\xi}-\xi\|~~~\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|\|\widehat{\xi}-\xi\|)

Furthermore, since
P[\delta\leq 1-\widehat{u}\leq 1-\delta]=1
for some
\delta>0
, and
R,E\in\{0,1\}
, we have that

\displaystyle\Biggr|\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\frac{RE}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}(\mu_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0})\biggr(\frac{u(1-\widehat{u})-\widehat{u}(1-u)}{1-\widehat{u}}\biggr)\biggr]\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\Bigr|\frac{RE}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}(1-\widehat{u})}(\mu_{0}-\widehat{\mu}_{0})\Bigr(u(1-\widehat{u})-\widehat{u}(1-u)\Bigr)\Bigr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon\delta}\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|\|u(1-\widehat{u})-\widehat{u}(1-u)\|~~~\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|\|\widehat{u}-u\|)

Finally

\displaystyle\Biggr|\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[(1-A)\frac{(\eta_{0}-\widehat{\eta}_{0})}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}\Bigr(Y(\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma)\Bigr)\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\Bigr|\frac{(1-A)Y}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}(\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0})(\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma)\Bigr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[|Y|\Bigr|(\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0})(\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma)\Bigr|\biggr]~~~(|1-A|\leq 1,\ \widehat{\eta}_{1}>\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{\epsilon}\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\|\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma\|~~~\text{($|Y|\leq M$ and Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\|\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma\|)

\displaystyle\Biggr|\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[-(1-A)\frac{(\eta_{0}-\widehat{\eta}_{0})}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}(\widehat{\chi}-\chi)\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[\Bigr|\frac{(1-A)}{\widehat{\eta}_{1}}(\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0})(\widehat{\chi}-\chi)\Bigr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\|\widehat{\chi}-\chi\|~~~\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\|\widehat{\chi}-\chi\|)

Combining pieces we have that

\displaystyle R_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)=O_{P}\biggr(\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|\|\widehat{u}-u\|+\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\xi}-\xi\|\Bigr\}+\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma\|+\|\widehat{\chi}-\chi\|\Bigr\}\biggr)

and thus

R_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)+R_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)=O_{P}\biggr(\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|\|\widehat{u}-u\|+\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\xi}-\xi\|\Bigr\}+\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma\|+\|\widehat{\chi}-\chi\|\Bigr\}\biggr)

Remark on nested nuisance functions.
In Algorithm 1, the regression targets for
\widehat{\xi}
,
\widehat{\gamma}
and
\widehat{\chi}
are built from
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
and
\widehat{u}
evaluated on the same training split used to fit them. We do not use nested cross-fitting for these functions, since it is considerably more computationally intensive. Because
\xi
,
\gamma
and
\chi
are smooth functionals of
\mu_{0}
and
u
(and
E\in\{0,1\}
,
\delta\leq 1-\widehat{u}\leq 1-\delta
), the error in each nested function is bounded, up to constants, by the error of its second-stage regression plus
\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|
or
\|\widehat{u}-u\|
. The bound in Corollary 3.1 therefore holds under the additional assumption that the second-stage regression errors
\|\widehat{\xi}-\xi_{\widehat{\mu}_{0}}\|
,
\|\widehat{\gamma}-\gamma_{\widehat{u}}\|
and
\|\widehat{\chi}-\chi_{\widehat{\mu}_{0},\widehat{u}}\|
are not inflated by the in-sample fit of
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
and
\widehat{u}
, which is reasonable for regularized learners. Here
\xi_{\widehat{\mu}_{0}}(\bm{L}^{*},Y)=\mathbb{E}_{P}[E\widehat{\mu}_{0}(\bm{L}^{*},\bm{L}^{e}_{m})~|~\bm{L}^{*},Y,A=1,R=1]
is the regression function of the fitted target with
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
held fixed, and
\gamma_{\widehat{u}}
,
\chi_{\widehat{\mu}_{0},\widehat{u}}
are defined analogously. The additional products of
\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|
and
\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|
with
\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|
and
\|\widehat{u}-u\|
are
o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
under the same
o_{P}(n^{-1/4})
conditions as the rest of the corollary.

S3.5
Summary of Asymptotic Behavior of
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
,
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
, and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}

In this section, we briefly summarize the asymptotic behavior of
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
, as well as
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
. We omit formal proofs, but note that justification of results in this section are nearly identical to the proofs of Theorem 3 and Corollary 3.1.

Asymptotic Behavior of
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}

Lemma S3

If
\|\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}-\dot{\alpha}_{P}\|=o_{P}(1)
,
\|\dot{\beta}_{\widetilde{P}}-\dot{\beta}_{P}\|=o_{P}(1)
,
\alpha(P)>0
, and
P\Bigr[\bigr|\mathbb{P}_{n}\bigr(\dot{\alpha}_{\widehat{P}}(O)\bigr)\bigr|\geq\epsilon\Bigr]\to 1
as
n\to\infty
for some
\epsilon>0
, then

\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathrm{EIF}}-\theta(P)=\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\theta}^{*}_{P}(O)]+O_{P}\Bigr(|R_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)|+|\widetilde{R}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)|\Bigr)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

where remainder term, omitting inputs for brevity, is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\Bigr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\Bigr)\Bigr(Y(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1})-(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}-\xi)\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{RE(\mu_{0}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0})}{\lambda_{1}\eta_{1}\pi+\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}(1-\pi)}\biggr\{\lambda_{1}\pi\Bigr(\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}-1\Bigr)+(\lambda_{1}\pi-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{})+\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}\Bigr(1-\frac{\eta_{0}\lambda_{0}(1-\pi)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{})}\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle+(1-A)\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}{1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}\cdot\frac{\eta_{0}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}}\biggr\{Y(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}^{\prime}-\gamma^{\prime})-(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}^{\prime}-\chi^{\prime})\biggr\}\biggr]

Moreover, if
|R_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)|+|\widetilde{R}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
then
\sqrt{n}\bigr(\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathrm{EIF}}-\theta(P)\bigr)\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}\bigr(0,\mathrm{Var}_{P}[\dot{\theta}^{*}_{P}(O)]\bigr)
, attaining the semiparametric efficiency bound induced by Assumption 4.

Corollary S3.1

Under the conditions of Lemma
S3
and assuming that
P(\delta\leq 1-\widehat{\pi}\leq 1-\delta)=1
for some
\delta>0
,
P(\widehat{\eta}_{a}>\epsilon)=1
for some
\epsilon>0,a\in\{0,1\}
,
P(\widehat{\lambda}_{0}>c)=1
for some
c>0
and
|Y|\leq M<\infty
almost surely

\displaystyle R_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)+\widetilde{R}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)

\displaystyle=O_{P}\biggr(\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\xi}-\xi\|\Bigr\}+\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime}-\gamma^{\prime}\|+\|\widehat{\chi}^{\prime}-\chi^{\prime}\|\Bigr\}

\displaystyle+\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\lambda}_{1}-\lambda_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\pi}-\pi\|+\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|+\|\widehat{\lambda}_{0}-\lambda_{0}\|\Bigr\}\biggr)

Asymptotic Behavior of
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}

Analogous to Corollary 2.1, we note that an influence function for
\theta(P)
follows directly from
\dot{\alpha}_{P}^{\prime}(O)
and
\dot{\beta}_{P}^{\prime}(O)
by
\dot{\theta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)=\frac{1}{\alpha(P)}\bigr(\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{P}(O)-\theta(P)\dot{\alpha}^{\prime}_{P}(O)\bigr)
.

Lemma S4

If
\|\dot{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\widehat{P}}-\dot{\alpha}^{\prime}_{P}\|=o_{P}(1)
,
\|\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{\widehat{P}}-\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{P}\|=o_{P}(1)
,
\alpha(P)>0
, and
P\Bigr[\bigr|\mathbb{P}_{n}\bigr(\dot{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\widehat{P}}(O)\bigr)\bigr|\geq\epsilon\Bigr]\to 1
as
n\to\infty
for some
\epsilon>0
, then

\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{IF}}-\theta(P)=\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\theta}^{\prime*}_{P}(O)]+O_{P}\Bigr(|R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)|+|R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)|\Bigr)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

Moreover, if
|R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)|+|R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
then
\sqrt{n}\bigr(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathrm{IF}}-\theta(P)\bigr)\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}\bigr(0,\mathrm{Var}_{P}[\dot{\theta}^{\prime*}_{P}(O)]\bigr)

Corollary S4.1

Under the conditions of Lemma
S4
and Corollary 3.1

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)+R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)

\displaystyle=O_{P}\biggr(\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\nu}-\nu\|+\|\widehat{\omega}_{1}-\omega_{1}\|\Bigr\}+\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|\|\widehat{u}-u\|\biggr)

Asymptotic Behavior of
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}

Lemma S5

If
\|\dot{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\widehat{P}}-\dot{\alpha}^{\prime}_{P}\|=o_{P}(1)
,
\|\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{P}}-\dot{\beta}^{\prime}_{P}\|=o_{P}(1)
,
\alpha(P)>0
, and
P\Bigr[\bigr|\mathbb{P}_{n}\bigr(\dot{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\widehat{P}}(O)\bigr)\bigr|\geq\epsilon\Bigr]\to 1
as
n\to\infty
for some
\epsilon>0
, then

\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathrm{IF}}-\theta(P)=\mathbb{P}_{n}[\dot{\theta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)]+O_{P}\Bigr(|R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)|+|\widetilde{R}^{\prime}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)|\Bigr)+o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

where remainder term, omitting inputs for brevity, is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}^{\prime}_{\beta}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{P},P)=\mathbb{E}_{P}\biggr[A\Bigr(1-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}\Bigr)(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}-\nu)+\frac{RE(\mu_{0}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0})}{\lambda_{1}\eta_{1}\pi+\lambda_{0}\eta_{0}(1-\pi)}\biggr\{\lambda_{1}\pi\Bigr(\frac{\eta_{1}}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}}-1\Bigr)+(\lambda_{1}\pi-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{})+\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{1}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}\Bigr(1-\frac{\eta_{0}\lambda_{0}(1-\pi)}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{0}(1-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{})}\Bigr)\biggr\}\biggr]

Moreover, if
|R^{\prime}_{\alpha}|+|\widetilde{R}^{\prime}_{\beta}|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
then
\sqrt{n}\bigr(\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathrm{IF}}-\theta(P)\bigr)\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}\bigr(0,\mathrm{Var}_{P}[\dot{\theta}_{P}^{\prime*}(O)]\bigr)
.

Corollary S5.1

Under the conditions of Lemma
S5
and Corollary
S3.1

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)+\widetilde{R}^{\prime}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)

\displaystyle=O_{P}\biggr(\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\omega}_{1}-\omega_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\nu}-\nu\|\Bigr\}

\displaystyle+\|\widehat{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\|\Bigr\{\|\widehat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\lambda}_{1}-\lambda_{1}\|+\|\widehat{\pi}-\pi\|+\|\widehat{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|+\|\widehat{\lambda}_{0}-\lambda_{0}\|\Bigr\}\biggr)

There are a few comments worth pointing out regarding these asymptotic results. To begin with
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
have the same asymptotic distribution, and likewise with
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
. Of course, the asymptotic results rely on slightly different assumptions regarding respective von Mises remainder terms. Moreover, performance may not be the same in finite samples. Given the appearance of
\lambda
in remainder terms for both
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
, these estimators may be unrealistic in practice unless
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
is low dimensional and one is willing to entertain a parametric model for
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
.

It is also worth noting that
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
do not attain the semiparametric efficiency bound induced by Assumption 4. Nevertheless, studying the form of
R^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\widehat{P},P)+R^{\prime}_{\beta}(\widehat{P},P)
, there is some clear appeal to the estimator
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
. To begin with, consistency only requires that
\eta_{1}
and one of the typical ATT nuisance functions are consistent, even if nested nuisance function
\nu
is inconsistent. This is in contrast to
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
which requires
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{0}
to both be consistent if any nested nuisance function is inconsistent, which could be more challenging if there is significant differential missingness by treatment status, and/or interactions between
A
and
\bm{L}^{*}
. Altogether, the optimal choice of estimator may be problem specific based on which assumptions an analyst is most likely to entertain, as well as the complexity of component nuisance functions.

S4
Simulation Study

S4.1
Data Generation

To investigate finite sample performance of
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
and
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
we conducted a simulation study tied closely to our motivating bariatric surgery study. In this simulation study, interest lies in the effect of RYGB vs. SG
(A)
on percent weight change 3 years post surgery
(Y)
among patients with pre-diabetes or diabetes, defined as an A1C
\geq 5.7\%
(
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
). Covariates
\bm{L}^{*}
included health care site, sex, race, baseline BMI, age, smoking status, and eGFR.

To generate simulated datasets, we began by sampling covariate vectors
\bm{L}^{*}
from the observed distribution in the DURABLE data to preserve the complex correlation structure of
\bm{L}^{*}
. Simulated treatment, outcome, and eligibility status (both
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}
and
R
) were generated using sampled covariates
\bm{L}^{*}
and models for the nuisance functions tied directly to the likelihood factorization in Equation (2) (
\pi,\eta,\lambda,\mu
). In particular, parametric models were fit on a sample of 16,461 surgical patients from the DURABLE database to inform this simulated data generation. This sample is larger than the analytic cohort of 14,809 patients in the data application, because it also includes patients undergoing surgery prior to 2008. Logistic regression was used for
\pi
and
\eta
, a gamma generalized linear model (GLM) was used for
\lambda
, and linear regression was used for
\mu
. The outcome model
\mu
was specified with several interactions between
A\times\bm{L}^{e}_{m},A\times\bm{L}^{*}
, and
\bm{L}^{e}_{m}\times\bm{L}^{*}
in order to introduce additional complexity and make
\mu
difficult to estimate well, perhaps even with more flexible machine learning methods.

Simulated data were generated following the factorization of the observed data likelihood according to the following steps. In the below steps, we let
\bm{\beta}_{f}
and
\bm{X}_{f}
respectively denote the coefficient vector and design matrix relevant to nuisance function
f(\cdot)
. Exact values of
\bm{\beta}_{f}
are presented in Table
S2
.

1.

Draw
\bm{L}^{*}
from the observed set of non-eligibility defining covariates in the DURABLE database.

2.

Sample surgery type
A~|~\bm{L}^{*}\sim\text{Bernoulli}\bigr(\pi(\bm{L}^{*})\bigr)
where
\text{logit}[\pi(\bm{L}^{*})]=\bm{\beta}_{\pi}^{T}\bm{X}_{\pi}

3.

Sample complete case indicator
R~|~\bm{L}^{*},A\sim\text{Bernoulli}\bigr(\eta(L^{*},A)\bigr)
where
\text{logit}[\eta(\bm{L}^{*},A)]=\bm{\beta}_{\eta}^{T}\bm{X}_{\eta}

4.

Sample A1C according to
(L^{e}_{m}-3)~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R=1\sim\text{Gamma}\bigr(\alpha_{\lambda},b(\bm{L}^{*},A)\bigr)
with rate parameter
b(\bm{L}^{*},A)=\frac{\alpha_{\lambda}}{\mathbb{E}_{P}[L^{e}_{m}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R=1]}
, and we parameterize
\log(\mathbb{E}_{P}[L^{e}_{m}~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R=1])=\bm{\beta}_{\lambda}^{T}\bm{X}_{\lambda}

5.

Sample 3 year weight loss
Y~|~\bm{L}^{*},A,R=1,L^{e}_{m}\sim\mathcal{N}\bigr(\mu_{A}(\bm{L}^{*},L^{e}_{m}),\sigma_{y}^{2}\bigr)
where
\mu_{A}(\bm{L}^{*},L^{e}_{m})=\bm{\beta}_{\mu}^{T}\bm{X}_{\mu}

6.

For subjects with
R=0
, set the value of
L^{e}_{m}
to be missing (
NA
).

1,000 simulated datasets were generated for each of
n=10,000
and
n=25,000
patients. These sample sizes are reasonable in the context many EHR-based settings, including our study of bariatric surgery for T2DM outcomes (
n=14,809
), the work of McTigue et al. (
n=9,710
) as well as previous DURABLE studies
(n>30,000)

(
Li et al., 2021
;
Arterburn et al., 2020
)
. Across simulated datasets, 62% of patients were treated with RYGB (
A=1
) and 70% of patients had ascertainable eligibility (
R=1
), of whom 57% were eligible (i.e.,
P(E=1~|~R=1)
).

S4.2
Estimators

When implementing
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{CC}}
, we only estimated
\mu
with the correct parametric specification, so that any resulting bias was due to selection bias and not model misspecification. When estimating
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IWOR}}
, we considered versions where both
\mu
and
\eta
were correctly specified, as well as versions where only one of the two was correctly specified. For
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IWOR}}
and the two influence function-based estimators, we applied six nonparametric strategies to each for flexible estimation of component nuisance functions using ensemble regression learners from the
SuperLearner
package in
R

(
van der Laan et al., 2007
;
Polley et al., 2023
)
.

The six strategies arise from all combinations of two distinct sets of libraries included in the SuperLearner (SL) ensemble and three strategies for estimating the outcome regression among SG subjects,
\mu_{0}
. The first set of SL libraries (SL1) included random forest (RF) learners with several hyperparameter combinations, along with learners for linear model (LM)/GLM, generalized additive model (GAM) (for binary variables), and multivariate adaptive polynomial regression spline (Polymars). The second set (SL2) dropped LM/GLM learners to test estimator behavior when the correct parametric model was not directly among the family of candidate learners.

Figure S1:
Estimates of
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
on a single simulated dataset across 6 nonparametric strategies compared with estimates of
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
from the correctly specified parametric model. Points are stratified by surgical site and self-reported smoking status, two covariates in
\bm{L}^{*}
which have interactions with surgical procedure
A
in
\mu
.

Given the complexity of interactions in the outcome model, we considered stratification (e.g., estimating
\mu_{0}
only on subjects with
A=0
) and augmenting the design matrix with all
A\times\bm{L}
interactions as alternatives to fitting a single outcome model
\mu
on all complete cases. The rationale is that fitting a single outcome model might inadequately capture the full scope of interactions in
\mu
, and thus there could be some degree of model misspecification, which may affect various estimators in different ways.

Figure
S1
illustrates estimates of
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
on a single simulated dataset across the six flexible strategies in comparison to estimates of
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
from the correctly specified parametric model. Clearly there is some variability in how well these strategies can estimate the complex form of
\mu
, and thus perhaps the performance of different estimators for
\theta(P)
.

S4.3
Results

Table
S1
presents relative bias and standard errors for all estimators, as well as coverage of 95% confidence intervals for the two influence function-based estimators,
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
and
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
. Unsurprisingly,
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{CC}}
was biased by over 15%, even with correctly specified outcome model
\mu
, thereby demonstrating that selection bias can be a prominent concern when discarding subjects with incomplete eligibility information. Performance of
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IWOR}}
was very dependent on model specification. When
\mu
and
\eta
were both correctly specified,
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IWOR}}
was unbiased, but when either nuisance function was misspecified the resulting biases of 17% and 24%, respectively, were worse than that of
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{CC}}
. Using nonparametric SuperLearner ensembles to estimate
\mu,\eta
did not guarantee unbiasedness, with plug-in bias ranging from 2-19%, with bias worse in cases with greater estimation error for
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
(Figure
S1
).

on

together (2) Fit
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
on

only (stratification) (3) Specify all

\widehat{\mu}

Correctly specified parametric models for
\mu
and/or
\eta

Table S1:
Comparison of estimators of
\theta(P)
in simulation study

Both influence function-based estimators were unbiased in all six nonparametric estimation strategies and attained nominal coverage for 95% confidence intervals, despite a degree of error in estimating
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
, and possibly other nuisance functions. Under each strategy,
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
had a standard error between 4-14% larger than the corresponding standard error of
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
, demonstrating the additional modeling complexities did indeed increase efficiency. With correctly specified parametric models for
\mu,\eta
, the standard error of
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IWOR}}
could at best match the smallest standard error among the
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
estimation strategies, and did not beat it: it matched it for
n=10,000
, and was 5% larger for
n=25,000
.

Altogether, these simulations demonstrate that both
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
and
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
are implementable with flexible, nonparametric estimation strategies for component nuisance functions, even in the presence of complex nested nuisance functions. The estimators retain good finite sample properties for sample sizes typical of EHR-studies for bariatric surgery. Finally, these simulations demonstrate that
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{EIF}}
attains noticeable efficiency gains if one is willing to estimate slightly more complex nuisance functions (cf.
\widehat{\theta}_{\text{IF}}
).
